  that would record it. Missing evidence stays an unknown; no value is guessed.
- **AFP-V0-388:** (accepted under the owner's standing auto-accept (2026-10-11; GH #1640))
  When no `--playwright-discovery` receipt is given, the `playwright:discovery-unproven` unknown's
  detail MUST start `MISSING:` and name the absent receipt and the
  `corvint affected discovery --playwright-config PATH --playwright-list FILE` command that writes
  it, since that input alone empties the selection. Scope, fallback and selection are unchanged.
- **AFP-V0-389:** (accepted under the owner's standing auto-accept (2026-10-11; GH #1604))
  In the general TypeScript adapter (no `--playwright-config`), a test of a runner other than
  Playwright MUST NOT keep tsconfig `paths` aliases unresolved (TJAA-V0-037/050) when no module
  in its relative-import closure names a bare reference the nearest `tsconfig.json` can claim.
  The test stays a blocker when any closure module has an unparsed, dynamic or unresolved load
  (including a relative import that resolves to no observed module), names a workspace package, or sits under an invalid nearest `tsconfig.json`, and a runner
  config (such as a Jest config) still blocks as before. Falsifier: an exempt test whose closure
  names a claimed alias, or an exemption under an opaque closure module.
- **AFP-V0-404:** (accepted under the owner's standing auto-accept (2026-10-11; GH #1604))
  In the general TypeScript adapter, a Playwright config's membership markers MUST NOT raise
  `typescript:executable-config-unresolved` when the closed static profile resolves its
  membership and the general adapter agrees with it: the only markers are `testDir` and
  `projects:`, the parse leaves no membership unknown (identity, use-value and dependency
  unknowns decide which browser runs, not which files belong; an absent `projects` is one
  implicit project), no project has `testMatch` or `testIgnore`, and every observed file the
  profile assigns to a project is a Playwright test of that config in the general adapter. Any
  other config keeps the frontier. Falsifier: a config without the frontier whose profile owns a
  file the adapter classifies otherwise, or one with `testMatch`, `globalSetup` or a computed
  `testDir`. Rollback for AFP-V0-389/404: restore the immediate `FrontierConfig` raise in
  `observe` and drop the `aliasFree` argument of `playwrightOnlyRunners`.

### Playwright dotenv inputs and grep-omission verification (GH #1646, #1647)

- **AFP-V0-405:** (accepted under the owner's standing auto-accept (2026-10-11; GH #1646/#1647);
  amends AFP-V0-386) A config's dotenv use MUST NOT be a writer under AFP-V0-386 when it is a
  modelled load: the config's leading top-level statements (before any other config code) are only
  `'use strict'`, side-effect imports of other modules, import declarations, require bindings and
  dotenv `config()` expression statements; dotenv is bound as a default or namespace import of
  `dotenv` or a plain `NAME = require('dotenv')`, or called as `require('dotenv').config(...)`; and
  each `config()` argument is absent or an object literal with only a literal `path` (no escape,
  no `~`, a repository-relative path that path cleaning leaves unchanged apart from one leading
  `./`, so not `.env/` or `missing/../.env`, which dotenv passes to the filesystem as written, and
  that is not `.`, outside the repository or a `.vault` file), `override: false`, and literal `true`/`false` `quiet` or `debug`. An absent `path` is
  `.env`. The path is relative to the repository root, the plan argv's working directory. Such a
  load reads one statically known file and never overrides a variable already present. Falsifier: a
  modelled load whose file or override behaviour depends on a value not in source.
- **AFP-V0-406:** (accepted under the owner's standing auto-accept (2026-10-11; GH #1646/#1647))
  When a plan named a variable and the config's only possible writers are modelled loads, each
  load's file MUST be observed and recorded, in load order, in `plan.environmentFiles` with its
  `path`, `site` (`CONFIG:LINE` of the call) and `state`: `ABSENT` when no file exists, or
  `PRESENT` with the file's lowercase hex SHA-256 `sha256`. A present file is parsed after dotenv's
  CRLF/CR normalization in the one line grammar every dotenv release reads alike: blank and whole-line
  `#` comment lines are skipped, and every other line is `NAME=VALUE` (optional spaces or tabs
  around `=`; a `NAME` of word characters, `.` or `-`) whose value is unquoted, or wholly single- or
  double-quoted with nothing after the closing quote, with no `#`, backslash, backtick, control
  character or JavaScript whitespace other than space and tab; the last duplicate wins. A plan
  without such a load omits `plan.environmentFiles`. Falsifier: a recorded digest that is not the
  file's, or a parsed value that differs from dotenv's.
- **AFP-V0-407:** (accepted under the owner's standing auto-accept (2026-10-11; GH #1646/#1647))
  Each recorded variable of such a plan MUST carry `resolved`, the value the config reads after the
  loads: a variable `SET` by the caller stays as recorded; an `UNSET` variable takes the first
  observed file's value that names it (`source` `DOTENV`, `file`, `state` `SET`, `value` and its
  `sha256`), or stays `UNSET` when no file names it. The variable's own `source`, `state`, `value`
  and `sha256` remain the caller's observation. AFP-V0-385 and AFP-V0-387 then evaluate the
  resolved value, so an absent `.env` leaves the `--playwright-env` value standing. Falsifier: a
  file value that overrides a caller-set variable, or a resolution that differs from dotenv's.
- **AFP-V0-408:** (accepted under the owner's standing auto-accept (2026-10-11; GH #1646/#1647))
  Every other dotenv use MUST keep the AFP-V0-386 EXECUTION unknown, and its detail MUST name the
  call site as `CONFIG:LINE` or the file: a named import, a non-literal or other `config()`
  argument (including `override: true`, which older dotenv releases ignore), a call that is not an
  expression statement, any dotenv use after other config code, an existing or uncheckable
  `<file>.vault` (dotenv decrypts it when `DOTENV_KEY` is set), an unreadable, non-regular or
  non-UTF-8 file, or a line outside the AFP-V0-406 grammar (named by number). `dotenv/config`,
  `NODE_OPTIONS`, any other loader and `globalSetup` stay writers as in AFP-V0-386. Falsifier: a
  resolved executable under one of these uses.
- **AFP-V0-409:** (accepted under the owner's standing auto-accept (2026-10-11; GH #1646/#1647);
  amends TJAA-V0-021 (b)) For a listing omission, the producer MUST decide whether the source tags
  are literal: the config, the test file and their static import closure all read as UTF-8, are
  all in the source set the receipt's source digest binds (so editing any of them makes the
  receipt BINDING_MISMATCH), have only static loads that resolve to exactly one existing
  repository candidate (two candidates such as `tags.cjs` and `tags.js` are ambiguous) or to
  `@playwright/test`, `dotenv`, `dotenv/config` and the `path`/`os`/`url`/`fs` or `node:`
  built-ins, name in code none of the reflective globals and their aliases
  (`Object`/`Reflect`/`JSON`/`Proxy`/`eval`/`Function`/`globalThis`/`global`/`window`/`self`/`structuredClone`),
  the routes to them (`constructor`/`prototype`/`__proto__`/`__defineGetter__`/`__defineSetter__`),
  the enumerating or defining helpers
  (`values`/`entries`/`assign`/`defineProperty`/`defineProperties`/`fromEntries`/`getOwnPropertyNames`/`getOwnPropertyDescriptor`/`getOwnPropertyDescriptors`/`setPrototypeOf`)
  or the in-place array mutators (`push`/`unshift`/`splice`/`fill`/`copyWithin`), hold no `'tag'`
  string, identifier escape, escaped string property key, computed object key or method, computed
  member write, or member access by other than an integer or string literal, and every code `tag` is
  an object-literal key whose value is a literal `@\S+` string (no escape, at least one character
  after `@`) or an array of them. Those strings are the closure's literal tags. Any other closure
  is not literal. Falsifier: a literal closure in which a describe or config tag could hold
  whitespace or be `@` alone.
- **AFP-V0-410:** (accepted under the owner's standing auto-accept (2026-10-11; GH #1646/#1647);
  amends TJAA-V0-021 (b)) When the source tags are literal and each case's explicit tags (the
  reported tags less the title `@\S+` runs) are all among them, the reported tokens equal the grep
  title's parts, so a case that (b) excludes is excluded for certain: the omission MUST be accepted
  without `unverifiedUnits` and without the `playwright:discovery-omission-unverified` unknown.
  Patterns, titles or tags outside that rule keep (b). Falsifier: a verified omission whose case
  Playwright's grep keeps, or one whose sources hold a non-literal tag.
- **AFP-V0-411:** (accepted under the owner's standing auto-accept (2026-10-11; GH #1646/#1647);
  amends TJAA-V0-021) The `playwright:discovery-omission-unverified` detail MUST name the unit
  count and list the units as `project "P" test PATH`, at most ten, then `and N more`.
- **AFP-V0-412:** (accepted under the owner's standing auto-accept (2026-10-11; GH #1646/#1647))
  A `BINDING_MISMATCH` `playwright:discovery-unproven` detail MUST name each binding that differs
  (revision, config path, config digest, source digest) with the receipt's and the plan's values
  (object ids and digests as their first 12 characters) and say to regenerate the receipt at this
  revision with `corvint affected discovery --playwright-config PATH --playwright-list FILE` from
  a fresh `playwright test --list --reporter=json`. State, scope and fallback are unchanged.
- **AFP-V0-413:** (accepted under the owner's standing auto-accept (2026-10-11; GH #1646/#1647))
  A `grep` or `grepInvert` using a JavaScript-only regular-expression feature (lookaround, a
  backreference, a flag other than `i`, `m` or `s`, or any other construct outside TJAA-V0-021
  (a)) MUST NOT be verified by AFP-V0-410: the omission stays in `unverifiedUnits`, and a pattern
  static selection cannot read keeps the plan `STATIC_UNRESOLVED`.
- **AFP-V0-414:** (accepted under the owner's standing auto-accept (2026-10-11; GH #1646/#1647))
  The new `plan.environmentFiles` and `environment[].resolved` members are omitted when empty, so a
  plan without a modelled dotenv load is byte-identical to before; the `playwright-discovery/0`
  profile is unchanged, and a receipt written before AFP-V0-410 that lists `unverifiedUnits` stays
  valid and unverified. Rollback for AFP-V0-405 to AFP-V0-414: restore the `dotenv` writer branch
  in `playwrightEnvironmentWriter`, drop `playwright_dotenv.go`, `playwright_tag_sources.go`, the
  `sourceTags` argument of `classifyPlaywrightGrepOmission`, and `playwrightBindingMismatch` and
  `playwrightUnitList` in `playwright_discovery.go`.
- **AFP-V0-417:** (accepted under the owner's standing auto-accept (2026-10-11; GH #1660);
  amends AFP-V0-386) A loader name of AFP-V0-386 MUST count as a writer when it occurs as a whole
  identifier in the config's code, in a template substitution, or as a whole string literal that
  is a property key through which the config can read that property: a computed member key
  (`box['global']`), a computed property key (`{ ['loadEnvFile']: load }`) or a quoted property
  key (`{ 'loadEnvFile': load }`), since a destructuring pattern reads through either. Inside other
  string, template or regular-expression text, an array literal element (`['loadEnvFile']`)
  included, it MUST be data, such as a project or dependency named `'global-setup'`, but only while the
  config tokenizes and holds no reflective channel through which text could become code or reach
  a property the scan cannot see; otherwise every occurrence counts, as before. The writer detail
  MUST name the matched name and its `CONFIG:LINE` site. `globalSetup` stays a writer wherever the
  config names it. Falsifier: a config whose only `global` words are string data keeping the
  EXECUTION unknown, or a code reference to `global`, `globalThis` or another loader name, or a
  quoted or computed destructuring key naming one, resolving the executable.
- **AFP-V0-418:** (accepted under the owner's standing auto-accept (2026-10-11; GH #1660);
  amends AFP-V0-409) A static load in the tag closure whose bare specifier the nearest bound alias
  config resolves through `paths` or `baseUrl` to exactly one bound repository file MUST be
  followed into that file, with the resolution the dependency graph uses (TJAA-V0-012,
  TJAA-V0-037). The nearest alias config is the one Playwright reads: the `tsconfig.json` or
  `jsconfig.json` of the nearest folder above the importing file that holds either, the
  `tsconfig.json` when a folder holds both. Every `jsconfig.json` MUST be in the source set the
  discovery receipt binds, so editing one invalidates the receipt. It MUST stay unresolved, and the
  closure not literal, when no bound alias config claims the specifier, the nearest one is not
  valid (unreadable, malformed or using `extends`), the importing file is not `.ts` or `.tsx` and
  the nearest config does not set `allowJs` to `true` (a `jsconfig.json` defaults it to `true`),
  since Playwright then maps nothing, the target is missing or ambiguous, or the Playwright config
  names `tsconfig` (Playwright may then read another file). Falsifier: a verified omission whose
  alias target holds a tag that is not literal, or one followed through an alias config
  Playwright does not read or applies to that importer.
- **AFP-V0-419:** (accepted under the owner's standing auto-accept (2026-10-11; GH #1660);
  amends AFP-V0-411) When the tag closure of the first unverified unit is not literal, the
  `playwright:discovery-omission-unverified` detail MUST append where it stopped: the file and
  the first specifier that resolves to no single repository file, a load that is not static, a
  file that is unreadable or does not tokenize, a file outside the bound sources, or a file whose
  tag or construct is not literal. The receipt and the `playwright-discovery/0` profile are
  unchanged; the reason is recomputed at plan time from the bound sources. Rollback for
  AFP-V0-417 to AFP-V0-419: restore the loader-name loop in `playwrightEnvironmentWriter`, drop
  `playwrightEnvLoaderReference`, `playwrightEnvKeyString`, `playwrightObservedName`, the
  `allowJS` field, the alias argument of `playwrightTagFileExact`, `resolveAlias`,
  `exactOrStop` and the `stop` field, and the appended detail in `SelectPlaywrightWithEnvironment`.
### Windows production path logic (V1-2488)

The native Windows test run of V1-2441 (build-log entry
2026-10-23-windows-native-test-qualification) classed 38 packages as path or logic defects.
V1-2488 re-reads each one; build-log entry 2026-10-23-windows-production-path-logic records the
root cause and class per package.

- **AFP-V0-415:** (accepted under the owner's standing auto-accept (2026-10-11; V1-2488))
  Production code that is meant to run on Windows MUST treat Windows path and executable syntax
  as data, never as a refusal reason: (1) `projectpath.Relative` converts slash input and a
  dangling link's target to the native separator before its component walk, so a missing
  `missing/child` below the root is admitted on Windows as on POSIX, and resolves a rooted
  target without a volume (`\x`) on the link's volume, so it is never classified as below the
  link's parent; (2) `flowdocs.openDirectory`
  (AFP-V0-361) opens its first component and, for the root itself, the volume root with the
  volume kept (`C:\x`, never `\C:`); (3) `sharedpath`'s alternate object store is refused only
  for the platform list separator (`:` on POSIX, `;` on Windows, Git's `PATH_SEP`), a quote or a
  line break anywhere in it, so Git for Windows' `C:/.../objects` is one alternate; (4) `breakagemap`
  compares `rev-parse --show-toplevel` with the canonical checkout after `FromSlash` and `Clean`;
  (5) `gitstatus.Executable` already resolves through `exec.LookPath`, which honours `PATHEXT`;
  its Windows fixtures are `git.exe`, the only name `exec.LookPath("git")` matches there. Each fix
  carries a portable test and, for literal Windows forms, a `_windows_test.go` file that
  `make windows-vet` type-checks (AFP-V0-054). Test logic of the same class is repaired with
  them: JSON fixtures encode OS paths (`liveverify/gotest`, `playwrightruns`), a Go source
  fixture quotes its path (`analyzergo`), absolute fixture roots carry a volume
  (`dashboard/adapters`, `postmergemetrics`), the INDEX key is built with `ToSlash` before the
  `../../` trim (`specindex`), and the missing-file reason is the platform's own text
  (`tools/ci-shard-costs`). Non-goals: Windows support for 1.0 (`windows/amd64` stays UNSUPPORTED;
  scope question V1-2485), the test-helper `.exe` and POSIX-tool defects (V1-2490), skips for
  fail-closed tests (V1-2486), and case-insensitive path comparison. Failure modes: a store path
  holding `;` on Windows (a UNC host included) or `:` elsewhere is still `git-unavailable`; a
  `--show-toplevel` spelling that differs only in case still refuses binding. Falsifier: a Windows
  run in which one of the named tests fails for a separator, volume or `PATHEXT` reason.
  Rollback: revert the named functions and tests; no wire format, receipt or persisted state
  changes.
- **AFP-V0-416:** (accepted under the owner's standing auto-accept (2026-10-11; V1-2488))
  A Windows failure caused by a deliberate fail-closed platform stub (a `//go:build !darwin &&
  !linux` or `!unix` file returning an unsupported error, or `procgroup`'s
  `process-platform-unsupported`) MUST stay a refusal and MUST NOT be "fixed" by weakening the
  stub; the build-log entry names the stub per package. The Tasks wire `PathText` (`ParsePathText`
  in `internal/tasks/wire/primitives.go`, Tasks wire §2) stays a POSIX absolute path beginning
  with `/`: admitting `C:\` changes the persisted head and manifest format and waits on V1-2485.
  Rollback: none; this requirement records a refusal and changes no code.

### Cross-call graph reuse and batch paths

GitHub #1601 (V1-2473): on a Rails checkout of about 12,100 tracked files (4,200 Ruby) each
`affected --path P` cost about 11 s and 300 MB on a busy host, and repeated calls shared nothing.
A profile of a generated tree of that shape (build log `2026-10-23-affected-graph-reuse-gh1601`)
put the cost in the graph build: one open per Ruby file, and every spec re-reading and re-scanning
the helpers it loads, about 60% of all opens. These requirements are proposed (owner auto-accept
pending); they do not change any plan, wire member or exit code.

- **AFP-V0-390:** (proposed; V1-2473, GH #1601)
  `affected --cache DIR` MUST be the only way the plan path reads or writes a graph cache: without
  it nothing is read from or written to any cache location. DIR is named by the caller; it is made
  absolute, the symlinks of its nearest existing ancestor are resolved, and it MUST be refused
  with `invalid-arguments` before any worktree read (`--paths-from` included) when it is at or
  under the repository root, textually or because the root is the same file as it or one of its
  existing ancestors (a case-insensitive file system, a firmlink), has a `.git` component in any
  case, is at or under (textually or by the same-file test) the repository's Git directory or
  common directory as `git rev-parse --absolute-git-dir --git-common-dir` names them (a
  `--separate-git-dir` or linked worktree can put them anywhere; a Git directory that cannot be
  named also refuses), is empty or given twice, or is combined with `--snapshot` or
  `--playwright-config`. Every entry read and write MUST go through one opened handle on DIR:
  the deepest existing ancestor is opened, each missing component is created (mode 0700) and
  opened inside the handle above it, and every handle is rechecked, by the path the kernel reports
  for the open directory and by the file identity of that path and its ancestors, against the
  root and both Git directories, so a DIR replaced by a symbolic link into the repository after
  the argument check is refused and a replacement after the open cannot redirect the write. DIR
  MUST belong to the calling user and be writable by no other user, or the cache is neither read
  nor written. DIR holds at most one entry per repository root and language set, written mode
  0600 through a temporary file renamed over the entry, so a reader sees a whole old or new
  entry. Failure mode: a process that renames the already opened and checked directory itself
  into the repository (itself a repository write) is outside the check, which holds only at the
  moment each handle is opened. Invariant analysis (AGENTS.md invariant 4):
  `affected` stays a read command. The entry is derived state at a location the caller named
  outside repository content, never under `.corvint/` (whose private ledgers are fixed by
  `SOL-V0-001`, `SOL-V0-007` and `SOL-V0-010`), never in Git or trace state, never evidence, and
  never an input to ranking, authority or learning; it can only reproduce a graph a full build
  would produce (AFP-V0-391 to AFP-V0-393). No user cache directory is used by default and no
  daemon or watcher is started (invariant 7).
- **AFP-V0-391:** (proposed; V1-2473, GH #1601)
  An entry (profile `corvint-affected-graph-cache/0`) MUST bind: the absolute repository root; the
  SHA-256 of the running corvint executable, so a build whose plugins may observe differently never
  reuses another build's entry (on linux `/proc/self/exe`, the running image itself; on darwin the
  executable pathname, accepted only when its change and modification times are strictly before
  the process's start and unchanged while it is hashed, so a replacement installed after the
  start yields no identity rather than its own; a replacement installed between exec and package
  initialization, the earliest clock reading, is a darwin failure mode); the ordered language set; each plugin's observed units, frontier
  and unobserved directories with nil and empty lists kept distinct; the documentation
  declaration; the graph digest; and a stat stamp (presence, mode, size, inode, device,
  modification and change time, nanoseconds), taken just before the read, for every directory a
  source walk listed and every path a plugin read, statted or found absent. A directory's times
  move whenever an entry in it is added, removed, renamed or replaced, so its stamp pins the listing
  the walk saw, including a skipped directory appearing and a file becoming a symbolic link. A
  header line carries the profile and the SHA-256 of the body. A program identity that cannot be
  read disables the cache. A read, stat or bounded read that fails for a reason its stamp does
  not pin (anything but an absent path, a path that is not a regular file, or a file over its
  bound, e.g. an I/O or permission error) MUST leave the observation unpinned, so a transient
  fault never becomes a reused graph missing that file.
- **AFP-V0-392:** (proposed; V1-2473, GH #1601)
  An entry MUST be trusted only when it is opened nonblocking and the open descriptor is a
  regular file within the size bound (a FIFO swapped in cannot block), its header checksum and
  profile match, its body decodes with no unknown member, its root, program identity and language
  set equal this call's, its stamp list is complete for its graph (strictly ordered and unique, and
  stamping the root listing, the documentation declaration, every unit source and test file as a
  present regular file, and every directory above one as a present directory, so an emptied or
  stripped list cannot restat nothing and pass), every recorded directory and path restats to the
  identical stamp, and the graph rebuilt from the recorded plugin results through the same
  admission, reverse edges and digest computation as a full build has the recorded digest. Any
  other outcome (a missing, oversized, non-regular, truncated, corrupt, forged or foreign entry, an
  incomplete stamp list, a cache directory that is not private to the user, or any changed stamp)
  MUST fall back to a full build and replace the entry; a cache
  failure is never a command failure. No entry is written when a recorded path changed during the
  build, any recorded path changed less than two seconds before the build started (the window
  inside which a timestamp cannot separate two contents), a plugin walked a present subtree it
  does not stamp (the Swift `.maestro` walk), a language observed through `Units` rather than the
  recorded source took part, or the platform exposes no change time (anything but darwin and
  linux, where an entry is also never trusted). Failure mode: a write that restores both
  modification and change time and inode is outside the stamp; change time cannot be set by an
  unprivileged process, but a file system whose change time only mirrors the modification time
  (exFAT, some FUSE mounts) lets a same-size write that restores the modification time pass, and
  a clock set backwards is the operator's own fault, as for Git's index. The checksum is integrity,
  not authentication: a process that can write DIR can forge a self-consistent entry for a stale
  graph whose stamps match the present tree; that is why DIR must be private to the user, and the
  stamp completeness rule only bounds what a stripped list can omit (stamps of reads outside the
  unit source and test files, such as a Gemfile, are not derivable from the graph).
- **AFP-V0-393:** (proposed; V1-2473, GH #1601)
  With `--cache`, every plan form it admits (default, `--full`, `--require-clean`, `--base`,
  `--path`, `--paths-from`, provider and app-map forms) MUST write stdout byte-identical to the same
  call without `--cache`, whether the entry hit, missed or could not be written, and MUST leave
  `git status --porcelain --ignored` unchanged. The cache replaces only `affected.Build`: the
  worktree status, HEAD, range, ignored-path check (AFP-V0-094), prior read scopes, advice,
  selection and the AFP-V0-150 drift rechecks still run on every call. Nothing about the cache
  reaches the receipt.
- **AFP-V0-394:** (proposed; V1-2473, GH #1601)
  The Ruby plugin MUST read and scan each Ruby file exactly once per observation, a loaded helper
  included, however many specs load it: the source loop and the helper scan share one read, with
  units, edges, frontier and digest unchanged. Non-goal: concurrent Ruby
  reads; on the profiled host a pool of eight readers cut wall time by under 10% for three times the
  kernel CPU, which a busy host pays (build log `2026-10-23-affected-graph-reuse-gh1601`).
- **AFP-V0-395:** (proposed; V1-2473, GH #1601)
  `affected --paths-from FILE` MUST add to the `--path` set every nonblank LF-separated line of
  FILE (relative to the root unless absolute, opened nonblocking with the open descriptor held to
  a regular file of at most 4 MiB, so a FIFO swapped in cannot block), each held to the
  `--path` canonical-path rule and the 4,096-path bound, joined with any `--path` values, and MUST
  then plan exactly as `--path` with those values does: the same bytes, including `path_list`. A
  missing or non-regular file (a FIFO included), an oversize file, a leading byte order mark, a line ending in a
  carriage return, an invalid
  line, a file with no path, a repeated flag, a separated value beginning with `-`, or any
  combination `--path` refuses MUST refuse with `invalid-arguments`.
- **AFP-V0-396:** (proposed; V1-2473, GH #1601)
  `corvint help affected` MUST document `--paths-from FILE` and `--cache DIR`, state that one call
  with many paths builds the graph once so callers should batch, and state the cache's location
  rule, verification, two-second window, platforms and byte-identical output; the `affected` row of
  `docs/CAPABILITIES.md` MUST name the batch form and the cache.
- **AFP-V0-397:** (proposed; V1-2473, GH #1601)
  A repeated single-path call on an unchanged Rails-shaped tree MUST restore the verified graph
  instead of building it, and write the cold call's bytes. Acceptance evidence is measured, not
  asserted by a timer in tests: on the generated tree of the issue's shape (11,808 tracked files,
  4,000 Ruby) a warm `--path` call MUST be sub-second on the build host, with the cold and warm
  times, peak memory and host load recorded in the build log and the PR. The reporter's real
  checkout (GH #1601) is not measured here; its warm time is `NOT_OBSERVED` until rerun there.

Non-goals: no default cache location, no cache for `--snapshot` (already immutable Git input) or
the Playwright profile, no incremental graph update after a change (a changed input rebuilds the
whole graph), no cache of the status, advice or selection, no background process, and no change to
the plan, its digest or any exit code. Rollback: drop `--cache` and `--paths-from` from
`parseAffectedOptions`, delete `cmd/corvint/affected_cache.go`,
`cmd/corvint/affected_identity_*.go` and `internal/liveverify/affected/graphcache*.go`, remove the `Source.record` hooks and restore
`affected.Build`'s direct call; the Ruby helper memo reverts independently.

### Opt-in self-hosted shard runner (owner request 2026-10-24)

The eight `go-product-shard` jobs are the hosted critical path (about 26 minutes). The owner has a
Linux host with more cores than a hosted image. These requirements let the owner move only those
shards onto it, only for runs whose code the repository's writers authored, and switch it off
without a code change. Security rationale and host setup: build-log entry
`2026-10-24-self-hosted-shard-runner`.

- **AFP-V0-420:** (accepted under the owner's standing auto-accept (2026-10-11; owner request
  2026-10-24; no ticket)) `go-product-shard` in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` MUST run on the runner
  labels `self-hosted`, `linux`, `x64` and `corvint-box` only when the repository variable
  `CORVINT_SELF_HOSTED` equals `on` and the event is `merge_group`, or is `pull_request` whose head
  repository is this repository. Every other case MUST use `ubuntu-24.04`: the variable unset or any
  other value, a push to `main`, a fork pull request and any other event. No other job MAY read the
  variable or leave the hosted image, and the job, matrix and check names MUST NOT change, because
  required checks match by name. Non-goals: fork pull requests, pushes, `pull_request_target` and
  every other job stay hosted; the runner never posts or changes `ci-control-plane` or any commit
  status, which stay with `ci-control-plane.yml`. The routing expression does not defend against a
  fork pull request that edits `ci.yml` itself, because a `pull_request` run uses the pull request's
  own workflow file, and the variable does not gate that run: it can target the labels whenever the
  runner is registered, even with the variable unset. The repository's fork-PR approval policy and
  the runner's repository access are that boundary, so the owner sets approval for all external
  contributors before registering the runner (build-log entry). Failure modes: with the box offline
  or busy, the routed shards wait in the queue, for up to GitHub's 24-hour queue limit; the job's
  75-minute timeout starts only when a runner picks the job up. `go-product` stays pending. The
  operator then unsets the variable, cancels the queued run and starts a new one (a new push or
  queue entry), which is hosted. Falsifier: a push, fork or variable-off run whose shard job runs on
  a self-hosted runner, or a renamed required check. Rollback: unset `CORVINT_SELF_HOSTED`, which
  needs no commit, or revert the `runs-on` line. That routes this repository's own runs back to the
  cloud but does not revoke the runner: the security rollback is to remove the runner registration
  or its access to this repository.
- **AFP-V0-421:** (accepted under the owner's standing auto-accept (2026-10-11; owner request
  2026-10-24; no ticket)) On `runner.environment == 'self-hosted'` the shard MUST provision nothing.
  It MUST NOT use `sudo`, the package manager or a download into a system path. It MUST only verify
  the host: `/usr/local/go/bin/go env GOVERSION` equals the `go-version` pinned by the job, and
  `/usr/local/bin/rg --version` reports the job's pinned `RG_VERSION` and is the `rg` that the fixed
  product PATH resolves. Otherwise it fails with an `::error::` that names the missing host
  prerequisite. It keeps the hosted step's user-global Git configuration lines. The hosted
  provisioning step MUST stay byte-identical and run only off the self-hosted runner. Because the
  host persists between jobs, the shard MUST first empty `GITHUB_WORKSPACE`, never restore or save the
  shared Actions Go build cache, and build with `GOENV=off` in a `GOCACHE` and, by AFP-V0-430, a `TMPDIR` under `RUNNER_TEMP`. The
  runner empties `RUNNER_TEMP` for every job; as amended by AFP-V0-427 to AFP-V0-429, only the
  host-local seed, which only a passing merge-queue shard on `main` refreshes, carries Go build
  output from an earlier job into a later one. The runner's own job-end cleanup ends the processes it
  tracks, and `maintenance.auto false` keeps Git from detaching any. This is hygiene, not isolation:
  code a job runs can still write to the runner account's home, tool cache or runner installation,
  or leave a process that escapes tracking, and so affect a later job. A persistent box is
  therefore trusted only as far as every run that reaches it is trusted. Isolation needs a fresh
  environment per job that is destroyed afterwards, such as a just-in-time runner in a disposable VM
  or container, which is outside these requirements. Host prerequisites: the four
  labels; the pinned Go at `/usr/local/go`; ripgrep 15.2.0 at `/usr/local/bin/rg`; a C toolchain
  for `-race` with `CGO_ENABLED=1`; `jq`, `git` and `bash`; and Landlock ABI 2 or later for the
  AFP-V0-023 wrapper (`test-confine -probe`). Failure modes: host drift, such as a Go or ripgrep
  upgrade or a removed tool, fails the verification step, and the shard runs no test. A shard on the
  box without a usable AFP-V0-427 seed starts with a cold build cache. Falsifier: a self-hosted shard that runs `sudo`
  or reads a build output, workspace file or Go setting from an earlier job, or a hosted
  provisioning step whose script differs. Rollback: revert the three steps' conditions and the
  verification and clearing steps.
- **AFP-V0-422:** (accepted under the owner's standing auto-accept (2026-10-11; owner request
  2026-10-24; no ticket); amends the AFP-V0-040 retention rule) Box timings are not cloud timings,
  so a self-hosted shard MUST NOT retain its AFP-V0-040 `go test -json` stream. Where a hosted
  shard would retain it, the self-hosted shard keeps only the marker `shard-<n>.self-hosted` as
  artifact `ci-shard-costs-runner-<n>`. The marker is best-effort and continue-on-error. When any
  marker is present, `ci-shard-cost-drift` MUST abstain with `Abstained: <k> of N shards ran on the
  self-hosted runner, whose timings are not cloud timings (AFP-V0-422)` and report no drift. A
  lost marker still leaves fewer than N streams, so the report abstains. Non-goals: no box cost
  table, and no change to the AFP-V0-047 forward-row outcomes, which carry results, not timings.
  Failure mode: while the variable is on, pull-request and merge-queue runs retain no streams, so
  AFP-V0-022 refreshes and AFP-V0-041 slice regeneration need a hosted run. Falsifier: a drift
  report, finding or table refresh computed from a self-hosted stream. Rollback: revert the marker
  steps and the abstention branch.
- **AFP-V0-427:** (accepted under the owner's standing auto-accept (2026-10-11; owner request
  2026-10-24; no ticket); amends AFP-V0-421) The self-hosted runner MAY keep one host-local Go build
  seed per runner, `$CORVINT_BOX_GOCACHE_ROOT/go-build-race-<RUNNER_NAME>`, where the job env
  `CORVINT_BOX_GOCACHE_ROOT` (`/home/russell/ci-cache`, outside every runner's work tree) is the
  only place the root is named. The seed is keyed by runner, not shard index: Go's action IDs
  include the package directory, and each runner checks out under its own `_work` path, so a build
  from another runner cannot hit (measured below). The seed is a symlink to a sibling directory
  `<seed>.<run>-<attempt>-<shard>`. Only on the self-hosted runner, only where the race invocation
  runs (not DOCS or REUSE), and only for a `merge_group` entry whose base is `refs/heads/main` or a
  `pull_request` whose head repository is this repository, the shard MUST copy (`cp -a`, never a
  hardlink, because Go can rewrite an existing cache entry in place, and preserving mtimes keeps
  Go's 5-day trim of unused entries meaningful) the seed's target into its per-job
  `$RUNNER_TEMP/go-build` before the tests. Same-repository pull requests restore because they
  already run as the same host user with write access to the root, so a read-only copy grants
  them nothing new; it gives the box's pull-request shards the same warm build. The restore MUST
  NOT fail the shard: a `GOCACHE` other than the per-job cache restores nothing, and a runner name
  outside `[A-Za-z0-9_-]`, a missing root, a root that is a link, foreign or group/world-writable, a
  missing seed, a seed that does not resolve to `<seed>.*`, a target this user does not own, or a
  failed copy each leave an empty per-job cache; every case prints a `::notice::`. `-count=1` stays on every race
  invocation, so no test result is reused. Hosted shards keep the V1-1098 `actions/cache` steps
  unchanged and never read the seed. Falsifier: a hosted or push run that reads the seed, a seed
  linked rather than copied into the job, a restore that fails a shard, or a race invocation
  without `-count=1`. Rollback: delete `/home/russell/ci-cache` (the next shard starts cold), or
  unset `CORVINT_SELF_HOSTED`, or revert the restore step.
- **AFP-V0-428:** (accepted under the owner's standing auto-accept (2026-10-11; owner request
  2026-10-24; no ticket); amends AFP-V0-421) Only a self-hosted `go-product-shard` of a
  `merge_group` entry whose base is `refs/heads/main` and whose tests step succeeded MAY refresh its
  runner's seed; no pull request, push or failed shard refreshes it. The refresh MUST run
  `go clean -testcache`, create the root with mode 0700 when absent, refuse a root that is a link,
  foreign or group/world-writable, remove every `<seed>.*` except the live seed's target before
  copying (so an interrupted or failed refresh's copy never accumulates), copy the job cache with
  `cp -a` into `<seed>.<tag>.tmp` beside the seed (removed again if the step fails), rename it to
  `<seed>.<tag>`, and swap it in by atomically renaming a new relative symlink over `<seed>`, so a
  reader never resolves a partial seed; it then removes every other `<seed>.*`. The size cap is explicit:
  `SEED_CAP_KIB` (8388608 KiB, 8 GiB) per seed; a job cache above it removes the seed instead, so
  the next shard on that runner starts cold and regrows only what it builds. With eight runners the
  root holds at most 64 GiB at rest, plus one transient copy per refreshing runner; Go's own trim
  drops entries unused for five days. A refusal or failure never fails the shard
  (`continue-on-error`). Each runner runs one job at a time, so its seed has one writer. Falsifier:
  a seed refreshed by a pull-request, push, non-`main` or failed shard, a partially copied seed
  that a restore resolves, or a seed above the cap after a refresh. Rollback: revert the refresh
  step and delete the root.
- **AFP-V0-429:** (accepted under the owner's standing auto-accept (2026-10-11; owner request
  2026-10-24; no ticket)) The seed's provenance is only as strong as AFP-V0-421's trust in every job
  that reaches the box. The event conditions keep honest pull-request builds out of the seed; they
  are not a boundary. Every job runs as the runner account, which owns the root, and a
  `pull_request` run uses the pull request's own `ci.yml`, so any same-repository pull request, or
  a fork pull request the owner approved, can write or replace a seed directly, and a planted entry
  is then compiled into a later merge-queue run, whose result could be wrong. Fork pull requests
  reach the runner only through the owner's approval (AFP-V0-420). Go's cache checks only an
  entry's size, not its content, so a corrupt seed may show as a compile, link or test failure on
  that runner, or may silently change what the tests exercise: `-count=1` stops reuse of test
  results, not of compiled code. On a failure or any doubt about the seed, the operator deletes `$CORVINT_BOX_GOCACHE_ROOT` (or that runner's `<seed>` and `<seed>.*`)
  and the next shard rebuilds cold. Non-goals: no seed is shared between runners or with the hosted
  image, no seed is a cache of test results, and the seed adds no isolation. Falsifier: a document
  or comment that calls the seed's event gating an isolation or security boundary.
- **AFP-V0-430:** (accepted under the owner's standing auto-accept (2026-10-11; owner request
  2026-10-24; no ticket); amends AFP-V0-421) On the self-hosted runner only, the verification step
  MUST create `$RUNNER_TEMP/tmp` and export `TMPDIR` to it through `$GITHUB_ENV` before the tests
  step, so Go's work directories and every `t.TempDir()` land on the box's ext4 work disk rather
  than its 16 GiB RAM-backed `/tmp` tmpfs, matching the hosted runners' ext4 temporary directory.
  No hosted step sets `TMPDIR`. The directory is outside the confined checkout, which AFP-V0-023
  grants as a path outside the confinement root. Cleanup and trust boundary: the runner empties
  `RUNNER_TEMP` for every job and runs one job at a time, so a file a test leaves is removed before
  the next job on that runner; this is per-job hygiene, not isolation, since every job runs as the
  same account (AFP-V0-429). Falsifier: a self-hosted shard whose `TMPDIR` is unset or under `/tmp`, or a
  hosted step that sets `TMPDIR`. Rollback: remove the
  two lines; temporary files return to `/tmp`.

## Non-goals and authority

No provider modification; execution only through the explicitly admitted AFP-V0-013 driver; no watcher or daemon (invariant 7,
`GPK-V0-010`); no exclusion certificates as authority; no non-Go provider package lists; no
LPCV-V0 requirement is implemented or promoted by this slice. The fast tier (AFP-V0-011) is not the
push or release gate and MUST NOT replace `make gate` in any declaration until a 200-commit shadow
run shows no selected-set miss the plan did not mark `UNKNOWN`; a narrowed run proves nothing about
the packages it omitted (AFP-V0-004). The AFP-V0-040 cost-drift report is advisory only: no
required check, commit status, automatic table refresh or commit, and no effect on shard
membership or placement.

AFP-V0-382 to AFP-V0-388 do not read any variable the caller did not name, do not model
`NODE_OPTIONS` preloads, shell wrappers, `globalSetup` or other process.env writers (each keeps
the unknown), and do not resolve the TJAA-V0-047 e2e-runtime-dependency frontier. AFP-V0-405 to
AFP-V0-408 model only the dotenv prelude loads they name: no `dotenv/config`, `dotenv-expand`,
`@dotenvx`, `process.loadEnvFile`, variable expansion, multiline or escaped values, encrypted
`.vault` files or a runtime `DOTENV_KEY`/`DOTENV_CONFIG_*` setting. AFP-V0-409 to AFP-V0-413 do not
evaluate computed patterns, titles or tags, run Playwright, or read tags through a package outside
the repository.
AFP-V0-389/404 do not change the `--playwright-config` mode or alias resolution for another
runner's loader.
AFP-V0-420 to AFP-V0-422 move no job other than `go-product-shard`, never run a fork pull request,
a push or `pull_request_target` on the self-hosted runner, never let it post a commit status, and
never provision the host, and do not isolate jobs from one another on a persistent host;
registering the runner and setting `CORVINT_SELF_HOSTED` stay owner steps. AFP-V0-427 to AFP-V0-429
share no seed between runners or with the hosted image, cache no test result, and add no
isolation; the seed root needs no owner step, because the first refresh creates it.

## Failure modes

Git unavailable or the worktree status exceeds its bound: fail closed with
`unsupported-affected-status`. No commits: `unsupported-affected-revision`. Source walk exceeds
`affected.MaxWalkEntries`, a plugin returns a non-canonical unit, or the walk accepts a source file
whose path no unit can name: `unsupported-affected-graph`.
An unreadable or invalid `--playwright-config`, or a Playwright graph that cannot be built:
`unsupported-playwright-affected`; dynamic but readable project/config semantics remain a typed
`UNKNOWN` plan with `FULL_RELEVANT_SUITE`, not a command failure.
Exhausting an admitted-directory sub-bound instead skips only that subtree and reports
`go:included-directory-walk-bounded` at `UNKNOWN` scope; it is not a graph refusal.
A dirty Tasks store projection path that no unit names is task state in `nonSource`, not an
unknown entry (AFP-V0-088). A dirty path owned by no plugin, or a changed unit (one that owns a changed path) with no
selectable test in any language (AFP-V0-020): the plan widens to `UNKNOWN` scope rather than
narrowing; the packages that name a dirty path by literal are added, never substituted for the
widening (AFP-V0-021). A potentially deleted or renamed-away Go path widens and selects its
package, or the importers of a package that is gone, rather than claiming no dependency path
(AFP-V0-004). An unreadable subtree:
`unsupported-affected-graph`, never a silently smaller graph. Worktree or HEAD changed during
compilation: `unsupported-affected-drift`. A `--base` that is not a full commit id:
`invalid-arguments`; one that is not a commit here: `unsupported-affected-revision`; a range diff
over its bound: `unsupported-affected-status`. `--require-clean` over a worktree with any status
entry: `unsupported-affected-mixed-worktree` (AFP-V0-045). In the fast tier every one of these, a plan the
script cannot read, a module-level Go frontier (including an invalid AFP-V0-023 read-scope
declaration or AFP-V0-037 binary-exec declaration), a dirty root module definition, or an empty
selection over a non-empty diff, or a repository the selector cannot index (AFP-V0-012) runs the
full `./...` command instead of a narrowed one, so the
worst case of `make gate-affected` is the cost of `make go-test`, never a skipped package.
The advisory cost-drift report (AFP-V0-040) abstains, with its reason in the job summary, on a
shard that did not succeed, fewer or more outcome artifacts than shards, an unreadable, failed,
empty, malformed, structurally invalid or unfinished package stream, or an invalid cost table; it
never fails CI on a finding, and a failure of any of its own steps, including the tool's build,
abstains with its reason in the job summary rather than failing the job.
In full CI, an unusable test-split allow-list, slice file or cost table keeps every package whole,
and a split package's catch-all slice runs every top-level test that no named slice names
(AFP-V0-041). A split package whose slice outcomes are failed, unterminated or not one per slice,
logs that are not one per declared shard, or a slice file that splits a package into more slices
than the declared shards, refuses the cost refresh and makes the drift report abstain
(AFP-V0-041 (8)). Shard logs that are not one per declared shard, or a shard log without any
terminal package outcome, refuse slice generation and replay (AFP-V0-041 (5)). The sharded PR
driver refuses, before execution, a placement that does not put every package or slice of the
runtime universe on exactly one shard (AFP-V0-048). Slice generation and replay combine a split
package's per-slice outcomes; any other repeated outcome, a test outside the slice its log ran,
or a slice that ends twice still refuses (AFP-V0-049).

Queue-backstopped PR narrowing failure modes (decision 0431): absent queue rule or unreadable
rules API, an allowlist miss or a new frontier force FULL; missing selection at queue time
produces `INCOMPLETE`; a queue timeout shorter than `CI` fails entries. Disabling the queue
forces FULL through AFP-V0-028. AFP-V0-026 retains the lingering control-plane status limit.
Late failures eject PRs; rerun passes classify flakes without changing the queue failure.
Artifact listing failure forces FULL; threshold trips and a reviewer-confirmed `CHANGE_CAUSED`
miss disable narrowing as AFP-V0-030 requires. Hosted auto-merge command compatibility is
`NOT_VERIFIED`, and net runner cost and ready-to-merge effects are `NOT_OBSERVED`.

`--path` failure modes (AFP-V0-094/095): a non-canonical value or an unsupported combination is
`invalid-arguments`, and so is a separated `--path` value that begins with `-`; a dirty worktree,
or an ignored untracked file the list names or the graph reads, is
`unsupported-affected-mixed-worktree`, never a plan that mixes bytes HEAD does not pin into HEAD's
graph; a listed path no language claims widens to `UNKNOWN`
rather than selecting nothing at `BOUNDED`.

`--playwright-env` failure modes (AFP-V0-382 to AFP-V0-387): a repeated or invalid name, or the
flag without `--playwright-config` or with `--snapshot`, is `invalid-arguments`; an unrecorded
variable, an empty value, an undefined value under a `channel`, an escaped literal, an
unmodelled expression or a possible writer keeps the EXECUTION unknown with a detail
that names the variable. A caller-supplied value that differs from the runtime's is the caller's
observation and is visible in `plan.environment`; it moves no test, because the executable is on
the EXECUTION axis. AFP-V0-389/404 fail closed: any unobserved load, workspace package, invalid
tsconfig, unmodelled marker or disagreement between the profile and the adapter keeps the
existing frontier. AFP-V0-405 to AFP-V0-413 fail closed: an unmodelled dotenv form, `.vault`
file, unreadable file or unmodelled line keeps the EXECUTION unknown and names the site or line,
and a non-literal tag closure (including an escaped or reflective route to a tag, an ambiguous
module extension or a dependency outside the bound source set), a reported tag the sources do not
hold or an unevaluable pattern keeps the omission unverified with its units named. A consumer still cannot read closure depth from an
`impact` receipt; only the help says so (AFP-V0-096).

Graph cache failure modes (AFP-V0-390 to AFP-V0-392): a `--cache` inside the repository, a
`.git` directory (in any case, or reached through a case-insensitive or firmlinked path) or the
repository's actual Git or common directory, a Git directory that cannot be named, or an
unsupported combination, is `invalid-arguments`; a missing, damaged, forged, foreign, stale or
incompletely stamped entry, a FIFO or other non-regular entry, a cache directory replaced by a
link into the repository or not private to the user, an unwritable cache directory, an
unreadable or replaced executable or an unsupported platform is a full build with the same
receipt, never a refusal and never a reused graph; an observation that cannot be pinned,
including one with a transient read failure, writes no entry. `--paths-from` refusals are `invalid-arguments`
(AFP-V0-395).
Self-hosted shard runner (AFP-V0-420 to AFP-V0-422): an offline or busy box leaves the routed
shards queued for up to GitHub's 24-hour queue limit, and the operator recovers by unsetting
`CORVINT_SELF_HOSTED`, cancelling the queued run and starting a new one; a registered runner stays
reachable by an approved fork pull request until it is removed; host drift fails the verification step with the named prerequisite; a
self-hosted run makes the cost-drift report abstain. A missing, unsafe or unreadable host-local seed
(AFP-V0-427) leaves a cold cache with a notice; a refresh above the cap removes the seed; a corrupt
seed may fail that runner's shards or pass silently, and the operator deletes the seed root on any
doubt (AFP-V0-429). A test that fills the per-job `TMPDIR` (AFP-V0-430) fills the box's work disk
rather than its memory, and the runner removes it with `RUNNER_TEMP` at the next job.

## Acceptance evidence and traceability

| Requirement | Implementation | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| AFP-V0-001 | `cmd/corvint/affected.go` `compileAffected` | `TestAffectedCleanTreeSelectsNothingAndWritesNothing` compares `git status --porcelain --ignored` before and after |

Compatibility and drift: the provider bundle grammar is consumed, not redefined; if
`go-live-test-provider-v0.md` changes its pattern grammar or bound, `providerMaxPackagePatterns`
and this spec change in the same commit. Unresolved decisions: whether a future composer consumes
this wire directly or the provider bundle grows a signed selection field; which trigger (editor
task, pre-commit hook) invokes the command; whether non-Go projections are wanted.

Rollout: the command ships in the experimental Go candidate; no adapter calls it. Rollback: delete
`cmd/corvint/affected.go`, its test, the help entries, this spec, and its README/INDEX rows; for
the fast tier alone, delete `script/gate-affected.sh`, `script/gate-affected_test.sh`, `tools/gate-affected-select`, the
`gate-affected` and `gate-affected-test` targets, and inline `GO_TEST_COMMAND` back into `go-test`,
which leaves `make gate` byte-for-byte as it was. Also delete `RangePaths` and `DecodeNameList` in
`internal/liveverify/affected/dirty.go` when the range form goes.
Promotion or kill: promote only after the LPCV-V0 composer accepts this wire or replaces it; kill
if a shadow run over 200 historical commits shows any selected-set miss against the full suite that
the plan did not mark `UNKNOWN`. The fast tier may become a push gate only after that same shadow
run passes; until then it is an everyday narrowing whose fallback is the full run.

Protected workflow/ruleset status: **VERIFIED** (2026-09-19). The repository workflow and literal
pins alone do not protect their own control plane; decision 0320 establishes the admission on the
free plan with AFP-V0-016 and ruleset 23699808, whose settings and observed runs are recorded in
`tools/corvint-pr-tests/README.md`. Decision 0390 removes the ruleset's bypass actor and adds
`doc-gates`; that ruleset change is not yet observed. For queue-backstopped PR narrowing,
AFP-V0-028/029 replace the historical PASS prerequisite; hosted enablement is `NOT_OBSERVED`.
The following closed runtime describes historical replay, not queue-backstopped PR execution.
The runtime environment is an allowlist with exact recorded bytes, a fixed absolute Go PATH,
`/usr/bin/cc`, `GOENV=off`, `LANG=C`, `LC_ALL=C`, `TZ=UTC`, and exclusively owned HOME/TMP/cache
under `/tmp/corvint-pr-tests-runtime`. An existing runtime path is refused; owned runtime state is
removed on return; cleanup failure is reported with a nonzero exit. Output/runtime paths are
resolved through their nearest existing ancestors before creation and rechecked as real external
directories, so a symlinked parent cannot create inside the tested tree. Historical rows use that same fixed layout and reset HOME/TMP/GOCACHE immediately before each test invocation.
Stdout is capped at 128 MiB and stderr at 8 MiB; overflow immediately cancels the process group
and invalidates execution. Hosted log preview is capped at 1 MiB/256 KiB. Planner/selector drift
at launch falls back to full; Go/compiler/Git/environment drift fails safely. The frozen identity
records the test Git executable path, digest and version, because tests run Git and its
version-specific output changes their outcomes. The driver's own Git reads and the `go test`
process both keep `GIT_GRAFT_FILE=/dev/null`, so tests inherit the graft refusal in every mode. The `go test`
environment differs only by setting `advice.graftFileDeprecated=false` through `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`,
since naming a graft file makes current Git print deprecation advice into the combined output tests
read (build log 2026-10-01-ci-shadow-graft-advice). Freeze ignores grafts;
row reuse binds the execution source/environment and raw plan/audit hashes as well as outcomes.

Container control operations have a two-minute deadline within an 80-minute launcher ceiling;
the test operation keeps its 75-minute bound. Docker daemon logging is disabled and inspected,
so PID1 file descriptors cannot bypass the retained-output bound. Both historical and PR
container checkouts use `/work/checkout`; process capture cannot bypass limits via io.Copy.
Run export requires only its actual selection/execution/Go outputs, not historical row files.

## Bounded documentation CI exception (2026-09-29)

The owner requested implementing a qualified README presentation path after PR #348. The separate
[Documentation CI V0](documentation-ci-v0.md) contract defines the only documentation-specific
exception to AFP-V0-013/014's root race invocation. It requires trusted-base source, exact merge
binding, a closed document scope, independently reviewed source-bound consumer qualification and
retained documentation checks. All other mandatory checks and AFP-V0-016 control-plane acceptance
remain. (accepted (decision 0431); amended by decision 0431) It does not alter this planner's
UNKNOWN scope or enable general Go selection; AFP-V0-028/029 govern queue-backstopped general
Go narrowing without the historical campaign. Missing or stale DCI qualification runs FULL.
